People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Um... yes. Well, I think so. I really hope I don't sound paranoid or anything, I know how that comes across and I don't want you to think I'm crazy. 

But it’s just... when they make those little comments on the radio, it lines up exactly with what the computer system is tracking about my route changes. Like, I’ll take a different street to avoid traffic, and suddenly someone on the radio makes a joke about a driver taking the scenic route or going off the grid. 

I know people tell me it’s just normal oversight from dispatch, and I really try to believe them—I always try to be a good employee and I definitely don't want to cause any problems at work. But... I can just tell that certain other drivers are reporting on me to compliance. They're watching what I do and sending it up the chain. 

Does that sound totally unreasonable? I really hope I'm just overthinking it, but it just feels so directed at me.

I really hope I'm just overthinking it, but it just feels so directed at me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is actually a coordinated effort at work to watch and report on you, or could it just be a misinterpretation on your part?\n\nPatient: I... But... well, like I was saying before, it just feels so coordinated. \n\nThe way dispatch and compliance track the route changes on the computer system, and then those little radio comments follow right after... it's just too exact to be a coincidence. I really wish it was just me misinterpreting things, Doctor. And I always try to look at my own faults first, I promise. I want to be a good employee. But... well, like I was saying before, it just feels so coordinated. \n\nThe way dispatch and compliance track the route changes on the computer system, and then those little radio comments follow right after... it's just too exact to be a coincidence. But even when they say that, I can just tell that certain drivers are definitely watching me and reporting on me. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that probably sounds like I'm not taking feedback well or being difficult, and I really, really don't want to be that kind of person. Am I making too big a deal out of this? I really value your opinion on it, I just want to make sure I'm looking at this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you always been this suspicious of other people, or is this a new change for you?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not always. But... I can just tell that certain other drivers are reporting on me to compliance. They're watching what I do and sending it up the chain. \n\nDoes that sound totally unreasonable? I really hope I'm just overthinking it, but it just feels so directed at me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is actually a coordinated effort at work to watch and report on you, or could it just be a misinterpretation on your part?\n\nPatient: I... I really wish it was just me misinterpreting things, Doctor. And I always try to look at my own faults first, I promise. I want to be a good employee. But... well, like I was saying before, it just feels so coordinated. \n\nThe way dispatch and compliance track the route changes on the computer system, and then those little radio comments follow right after... it's just too exact to be a coincidence. I know I should just accept it when people tell me it's just normal oversight, and I really am trying to be a good sport about it, I swear. But even when they say that, I can just tell that certain drivers are definitely watching me and reporting on me. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that probably sounds like I'm not taking feedback well or being difficult, and I really, really don't want to be that kind of person. Am I making too big a deal out of this? I really value your opinion on it, I just want to make sure I'm looking at this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you always been this suspicious of other people, or is this a new change for you?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not always. I really try to be a trusting person, and I usually get along great with all my coworkers. I'd hate for you to think I'm just naturally suspicious or looking for trouble... I'm really not, I promise. \n\nIt's just... well, this specific thing with the tracking through the computer system and those radio comments, it's only really felt clear to me for about the past two months. Before that, everything was totally fine and I never had any issues with anyone. I just started noticing it recently, and... well, I hope it's okay that I brought it up. Is two months a normal amount of time to be stressed out about something like this?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other unusual experiences, like hearing or seeing things that other people don’t?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no... definitely not. I've never had anything like that happen. \n\nI mean, I know the radio thing I was talking about probably sounds a little strange, but it's coming from the actual physical radio in the truck, I promise. It's not voices in my head or anything like that. I really hope you don't think I'm losing touch with reality... I'm definitely not seeing visions or hearing things that aren't there. \n\nThat's a good sign, right? I just really want to make sure I'm giving you all the right information and not making you think I'm completely crazy.\n\nDoctor: Have you generally been suspicious of other people in most areas of your life, not just at work?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not. I really try my best to see the good in people, I promise. Outside of work, I don't have any issues like this at all. I have a good group of friends, and I get along with my family... I'm usually the one who trusts people a little too much, if anything. \n\nI'd hate for you to think I'm just a paranoid person who thinks everyone is out to get me. I'm really not like that. It's just this one specific situation at work that's been so stressful. I'm usually very easygoing. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining that right. I just want to be sure you know I'm usually very trusting.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Well, I... I mean, I try to look at it from all sides, I really do. I don't want to seem like I'm just jumping to conclusions or being unreasonable. But... it's just so hard to ignore what's right in front of me.

Well, I think so. I really hope I don't sound paranoid or anything, I know how that comes across and I don't want you to think I'm crazy. \n\nBut it’s just... when they make those little comments on the radio, it lines up exactly with what the computer system is tracking about my route changes. Like, I’ll take a different street to avoid traffic, and suddenly someone on the radio makes a joke about a driver taking the scenic route or going off the grid. \n\nI know people tell me it’s just normal oversight from dispatch, and I really try to believe them—I always try to be a good employee and I definitely don't want to cause any problems at work. But... I can just tell that certain other drivers are reporting on me to compliance. They're watching what I do and sending it up the chain. \n\nDoes that sound totally unreasonable? I really hope I'm just overthinking it, but it just feels so directed at me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is actually a coordinated effort at work to watch and report on you, or could it just be a misinterpretation on your part?\n\nPatient: I... I really wish it was just me misinterpreting things, Doctor. And I always try to look at my own faults first, I promise. I want to be a good employee. But... well, like I was saying before, it just feels so coordinated. \n\nThe way dispatch and compliance track the route changes on the computer system, and then those little radio comments follow right after... it's just too exact to be a coincidence. I know I should just accept it when people tell me it's just normal oversight, and I really am trying to be a good sport about it, I swear. But even when they say that, I can just tell that certain drivers are definitely watching me and reporting on me. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that probably sounds like I'm not taking feedback well or being difficult, and I really, really don't want to be that kind of person. Am I making too big a deal out of this? I really value your opinion on it, I just want to make sure I'm looking at this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you always been this suspicious of other people, or is this a new change for you?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not always. Is two months a normal amount of time to be stressed out about something like this?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other unusual experiences, like hearing or seeing things that other people don’t?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no... definitely not. I've never had anything like that happen. \n\nI mean, I know the radio thing I was talking about probably sounds a little strange, but it's coming from the actual physical radio in the truck, I promise. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, I know it sounds a little... intense, when I say it out loud. And I really don't want to seem like I'm just making things up or overreacting, because I'm not that kind of person. But... yes, I really do think it's happening. 

I'm here because... well, dispatch and compliance, they've been tracking my route changes through the computer system. And I hear these little comments on the radio, too. I just want to be sure you know I'm usually very trusting.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a similar belief that other people were watching, judging, or talking about you in the past?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, never. Nothing like this at all. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I guess if I really think about it, it's... it's been a bit longer than I first said. I'm sorry, I wasn't trying to hide anything, I just... I didn't want to seem like I've been struggling for too long.

Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, no, definitely not. With my close friends and family, I've always been an open book. I mean, I really value the people in my life, and I always want them to know they can trust me, just like I trust them. 

I've never had any reason to doubt my family or my close friends. I've always tried to be the kind of person who is supportive and easy to get along with. I wouldn't want to cause any friction in my personal relationships. 

Does that sound normal to you? I just want to make sure you know that I'm usually a very trusting person in my personal life. I really hope that helps clarify things.
